# Codex audit — Phase 5.5 V1 (LoopbackTransport) 2026-05-19

## Context

Working dir: `/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook` (engine worktree, `feat/quantbook-engine`).

HEAD: `924750819bc` (truncation-fix on top of `3590aa6d89e` Phase 5.5 V1).

## What shipped

Phase 5.5 V1 adds `LoopbackTransport` — an in-process paired `Transport` impl for 2-peer round-trip testing. The `Transport` trait already shipped at 5.2.a with only `NoopTransport` (returns None forever); 5.5 V1 closes the gap.

Single (effectively) commit `3590aa6d89e` + truncation-fix `924750819bc`.

Files changed:
1. `crates/ql-collab/src/transport.rs`:
   - New imports: `VecDeque`, `Arc`, `Mutex`, `AtomicBool`, `Ordering`.
   - New `LoopbackTransport` struct with `inbox: Arc<Mutex<VecDeque<Vec<u8>>>>`, `outbox` (same), `closed: AtomicBool`.
   - `LoopbackTransport::pair() -> (Self, Self)` returns two endpoints where A.send → B.inbox and B.send → A.inbox.
   - Methods: `close(&self)` (atomic), `is_closed()`, `pending_recv() -> usize`.
   - `Transport` impl: send/try_recv lock the appropriate queue, error on lock-poisoned, error on closed flag.
   - 8 unit tests (round-trip both directions, bidirectional, FIFO, pending_recv, per-endpoint close independence, own-side close blocks send+recv, Debug).
2. `crates/ql-collab/src/lib.rs`: re-export `LoopbackTransport`; transport-module docstring updated.
3. `crates/ql-collab/src/session.rs`: new integration test `two_sessions_exchange_state_via_loopback_transport` drives two CollabSessions through a paired transport.

Workspace tests: 4187 → 4196 (+9).

## What you need to verify

### A. Concurrency soundness

A1. `LoopbackTransport` uses `Arc<Mutex<VecDeque<Vec<u8>>>>` for inbox/outbox + `AtomicBool` for closed. Is the struct genuinely `Send + Sync`? Verify by checking field types.

A2. `close(&self)` uses `closed.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed)`. `send`/`try_recv` check via `closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed)`. Is `Relaxed` correct here, or do we need `Acquire`/`Release` to avoid the close-before-send race where one thread closes and another reads the queue concurrently?

A3. `Mutex::lock().unwrap()` would panic on poison; we use `map_err` to return `TransportError::Io`. Is this the right semantics, or should poison be an unrecoverable panic at the test layer?

A4. Deadlock potential: each Transport method locks ONE queue. The two endpoints share the queue pair via two distinct Arc<Mutex<>>s — locking order is consistent (always self.inbox OR self.outbox, never both). Verify no path locks both.

### B. API design

B1. `Transport::send` / `try_recv` take `&mut self`. `LoopbackTransport`'s internal state is all behind Arc/Mutex/Atomic — `&self` would also work. Is keeping `&mut self` defensible (matches NoopTransport + the trait contract)?

B2. `pair() -> (Self, Self)` returns two endpoints with NO shared closed-state, only shared queues. If A closes, A's send/recv error; B can still write into A's now-orphaned inbox. Is this the right semantic for "close" or should B also see a Closed error when trying to send to a closed-A?

B3. `close(&self)` is `&self` (atomic); `is_closed(&self)` is `&self`. `send/try_recv` are `&mut self` per trait. Consistent inconsistency or surprising?

B4. `Send + Sync` requirement: callers may want to use LoopbackTransport across threads (one peer per thread). Is the implementation actually Send+Sync? Add a static assertion test if not already verified.

### C. Test coverage gaps

C1. The 8 unit tests cover send, recv, FIFO, close, debug. Anything missing?

C2. No test for "send arbitrary-sized blob" (e.g. 1MB Loro snapshot). Acceptable for unit tests?

C3. No multi-threaded test using `std::thread::spawn` to verify Send+Sync at runtime. Worth adding?

C4. The session-level integration test `two_sessions_exchange_state_via_loopback_transport` covers bidirectional exchange + convergence. Anything obvious missing?

C5. No test for "B sends after A closes" — what should happen? Currently B's send succeeds (bytes orphan in A's inbox). Per the docstring this is intentional, but worth a test pinning that behavior.

### D. Doc accuracy

D1. `crdt-data-model.md` § Transport (Phase 5.5 preview) — needs "V1 LoopbackTransport shipped at 924750819bc" marker?

D2. `entry-plan.md` 5.5 sub-item row — currently "future"?

D3. `known-gaps.md` GAP-C-05 — was updated at 5.6 V1 closure to mention "Transport trait shipped but only NoopTransport impl exists". Needs another update to reflect LoopbackTransport.

D4. `session.rs:12-15` scaffold-overview Phase 5.5 bullet — still future-tense.

D5. `transport.rs:3-5` module-level docstring says "Phase 5.5 will implement (WebSocket or Server-Sent-Events or custom protocol)" — partially shipped now (LoopbackTransport is one impl); needs refinement.

### E. Gates

E1. `cargo test --workspace` — expect 4196.
E2. `cargo fmt --all -- --check` — expect clean.
E3. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` — expect clean.

## Output format

A1-A4 / B1-B4 / C1-C5 / D1-D5 / E1-E3 with verdicts. Findings table HIGH / MEDIUM / LOW + file:line + concrete fixes. End with "Phase 5.5 V1 is ship-clean" / "...N MUST-FIX before next session" / "...N SHOULD-FIX for cleanup". Be specific.

# VS Code Copilot Instructions

## Project Overview

Visual Studio Code is built with a layered architecture using TypeScript, web APIs and Electron, combining web technologies with native app capabilities. The codebase is organized into key architectural layers:

### Root Folders
- `src/`: Main TypeScript source code with unit tests in `src/vs/*/test/` folders
- `build/`: Build scripts and CI/CD tools
- `extensions/`: Built-in extensions that ship with VS Code
- `test/`: Integration tests and test infrastructure
- `scripts/`: Development and build scripts
- `resources/`: Static resources (icons, themes, etc.)
- `out/`: Compiled JavaScript output (generated during build)

### Core Architecture (`src/` folder)
- `src/vs/base/` - Foundation utilities and cross-platform abstractions
- `src/vs/platform/` - Platform services and dependency injection infrastructure
- `src/vs/editor/` - Text editor implementation with language services, syntax highlighting, and editing features
- `src/vs/workbench/` - Main application workbench for web and desktop
  - `workbench/browser/` - Core workbench UI components (parts, layout, actions)
  - `workbench/services/` - Service implementations
  - `workbench/contrib/` - Feature contributions (git, debug, search, terminal, etc.)
  - `workbench/api/` - Extension host and VS Code API implementation
- `src/vs/code/` - Electron main process specific implementation
- `src/vs/server/` - Server specific implementation

The core architecture follows these principles:
- **Layered architecture** - from `base`, `platform`, `editor`, to `workbench`
- **Dependency injection** - Services are injected through constructor parameters
- **Contribution model** - Features contribute to registries and extension points
- **Cross-platform compatibility** - Abstractions separate platform-specific code

### Built-in Extensions (`extensions/` folder)
The `extensions/` directory contains first-party extensions that ship with VS Code:
- **Language support** - `typescript-language-features/`, `html-language-features/`, `css-language-features/`, etc.
- **Core features** - `git/`, `debug-auto-launch/`, `emmet/`, `markdown-language-features/`
- **Themes** - `theme-*` folders for default color themes
- **Development tools** - `extension-editing/`, `vscode-api-tests/`

Each extension follows the standard VS Code extension structure with `package.json`, TypeScript sources, and contribution points to extend the workbench through the Extension API.

### Finding Related Code
1. **Semantic search first**: Use file search for general concepts
2. **Grep for exact strings**: Use grep for error messages or specific function names
3. **Follow imports**: Check what files import the problematic module
4. **Check test files**: Often reveal usage patterns and expected behavior

## Validating TypeScript changes

MANDATORY: Always check the `VS Code - Build` watch task output via #runTasks/getTaskOutput for compilation errors before running ANY script or declaring work complete, then fix all compilation errors before moving forward.

- NEVER run tests if there are compilation errors
- NEVER use `npm run compile` to compile TypeScript files but call #runTasks/getTaskOutput instead

### TypeScript compilation steps
- Monitor the `VS Code - Build` task outputs for real-time compilation errors as you make changes
- This task runs `Core - Build` and `Ext - Build` to incrementally compile VS Code TypeScript sources and built-in extensions
- Start the task if it's not already running in the background

### TypeScript validation steps
- Use the run test tool if you need to run tests. If that tool is not available, then you can use `scripts/test.sh` (or `scripts\test.bat` on Windows) for unit tests (add `--grep <pattern>` to filter tests) or `scripts/test-integration.sh` (or `scripts\test-integration.bat` on Windows) for integration tests (integration tests end with .integrationTest.ts or are in /extensions/).
- Use `npm run valid-layers-check` to check for layering issues

## Coding Guidelines

### Indentation

We use tabs, not spaces.

### Naming Conventions

- Use PascalCase for `type` names
- Use PascalCase for `enum` values
- Use camelCase for `function` and `method` names
- Use camelCase for `property` names and `local variables`
- Use whole words in names when possible

### Types

- Do not export `types` or `functions` unless you need to share it across multiple components
- Do not introduce new `types` or `values` to the global namespace

### Comments

- Use JSDoc style comments for `functions`, `interfaces`, `enums`, and `classes`

### Strings

- Use "double quotes" for strings shown to the user that need to be externalized (localized)
- Use 'single quotes' otherwise
- All strings visible to the user need to be externalized using the `vs/nls` module
- Externalized strings must not use string concatenation. Use placeholders instead (`{0}`).

### UI labels
- Use title-style capitalization for command labels, buttons and menu items (each word is capitalized).
- Don't capitalize prepositions of four or fewer letters unless it's the first or last word (e.g. "in", "with", "for").

### Style

- Use arrow functions `=>` over anonymous function expressions
- Only surround arrow function parameters when necessary. For example, `(x) => x + x` is wrong but the following are correct:

```typescript
x => x + x
(x, y) => x + y
<T>(x: T, y: T) => x === y
```

- Always surround loop and conditional bodies with curly braces
- Open curly braces always go on the same line as whatever necessitates them
- Parenthesized constructs should have no surrounding whitespace. A single space follows commas, colons, and semicolons in those constructs. For example:

```typescript
for (let i = 0, n = str.length; i < 10; i++) {
    if (x < 10) {
        foo();
    }
}
function f(x: number, y: string): void { }
```

- Whenever possible, use in top-level scopes `export function x(…) {…}` instead of `export const x = (…) => {…}`. One advantage of using the `function` keyword is that the stack-trace shows a good name when debugging.

### Code Quality

- All files must include Microsoft copyright header
- Prefer `async` and `await` over `Promise` and `then` calls
- All user facing messages must be localized using the applicable localization framework (for example `nls.localize()` method)
- Don't add tests to the wrong test suite (e.g., adding to end of file instead of inside relevant suite)
- Look for existing test patterns before creating new structures
- Use `describe` and `test` consistently with existing patterns
- Prefer regex capture groups with names over numbered capture groups.
- If you create any temporary new files, scripts, or helper files for iteration, clean up these files by removing them at the end of the task
- Never duplicate imports. Always reuse existing imports if they are present.
- Do not use `any` or `unknown` as the type for variables, parameters, or return values unless absolutely necessary. If they need type annotations, they should have proper types or interfaces defined.
- When adding file watching, prefer correlated file watchers (via fileService.createWatcher) to shared ones.
- When adding tooltips to UI elements, prefer the use of IHoverService service.

//! `ql-collab` — Phase 5 multi-user CRDT collaboration crate.
//!
//! **Phase 5.2.a (2026-05-19, scaffold ship):** establishes the
//! module shape per the audit-closed design at
//! `docs/architecture/crdt-data-model.md`. Phase 5.1 locked Option
//! A (preserve op-log shape on top of Loro `LoroList`); this crate
//! is the layer above ql-oplog that orchestrates multi-peer
//! collaboration.
//!
//! ## Module layout
//!
//! - [`peer`] — `PeerId` type. Stable per-session identifier used
//!   to attribute ops + presence state. Wraps Loro's `u64` peer id.
//! - [`session`] — `CollabSession`: per-peer state holder that
//!   wraps an `OpLog`, manages local appends, and exposes
//!   `merge_bytes` + `export_bytes` for transport integration.
//! - [`transport`] — `Transport` trait + 2 impls: `NoopTransport`
//!   (test stub, records sends + returns None forever) and
//!   `LoopbackTransport` (Phase 5.5 V1 ship 2026-05-19 —
//!   in-process paired endpoints for 2-peer round-trip tests).
//!   Phase 5.5 V2 will add a WebSocket impl.
//! - [`presence`] — per-peer ephemeral cursor + selection state
//!   (Phase 5.6 V1 ship 2026-05-19). `PresenceState` JSON-encoded
//!   into the shared `LoroDoc`'s `"presence"` LoroMap (LWW per key).
//! - [`undo`] — peer-local undo/redo via Loro's `UndoManager`
//!   (Phase 5.4 V1 ship 2026-05-19). `CollabSession` wires the
//!   manager + auto-excludes presence-origin commits.
//!
//! ## Stability
//!
//! Pre-0.2.0: the public API is in flux. Phase 5.2.b through 5.7
//! will populate the reserved modules and may shift trait shapes.
//! No `#[non_exhaustive]` on enums yet — locked at Phase 5.8
//! megaudit per the engine audit-discipline rule.
//!
//! ### Pre-stability API changes (logged)
//!
//! - **Phase 5.2.b (2026-05-19, `ef056f50bee`):** `CollabSession::new`
//!   return type changed from `Self` to
//!   `Result<Self, CollabSessionError>`. Loro's `set_peer_id` is
//!   fallible (`u64::MAX` is reserved), so the constructor must
//!   propagate the error. Internal-only at the time of the change;
//!   no external callers.
//! - **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19, `89c02b9d83e` + audit closure):**
//!   `CollabSessionError::Undo(loro::LoroError)` variant added.
//!   Exposes `loro::LoroError` in the public error surface — pre-0.2.0
//!   this couples consumers to the loro crate version; a stable API
//!   would wrap it in a ql-collab-owned error. Will be re-typed
//!   before 0.2.0.
//! - **Phase 5.4 V1 audit closure (2026-05-19):**
//!   `ql_oplog::OpLog::set_peer_id` signature changed from `&self`
//!   to `&mut self`. Prevents accidental peer-id changes through
//!   `CollabSession::op_log()` (`&OpLog`) which would silently clear
//!   an attached `UndoManager`'s stacks per Loro's internal
//!   subscription behavior (`loro-internal::undo:654-662`).

pub mod peer;
pub mod presence;
pub mod session;
pub mod transport;
pub mod undo;

pub use peer::PeerId;
pub use presence::{PresenceError, PresenceState};
pub use session::{CollabSession, CollabSessionError};
pub use transport::{LoopbackTransport, NoopTransport, Transport, TransportError};
pub use undo::UndoManager;

//! `Transport` trait — wire-byte channel for Phase 5 collaboration.
//!
//! **Phase 5.2.a (2026-05-19, scaffold ship):** defines the trait
//! shape that Phase 5.5 will implement (WebSocket or
//! Server-Sent-Events or custom protocol — the choice is 5.5's
//! call). Tests in the meantime use [`NoopTransport`] which
//! discards all traffic.
//!
//! The contract is intentionally minimal:
//!
//! - `send(&mut self, bytes: &[u8])` — push bytes to the channel.
//!   Bytes are an opaque blob (a Loro export). The transport
//!   doesn't interpret them.
//! - `try_recv(&mut self) -> Option<Vec<u8>>` — non-blocking
//!   receive. Returns `None` if no bytes are queued.
//!
//! That's enough to plumb a `CollabSession::export_bytes` →
//! transport → remote `CollabSession::merge_bytes` flow. The
//! transport handles framing / reliability / reconnect / auth as
//! its implementation details.

use std::collections::VecDeque;
use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, Ordering};
use std::sync::{Arc, Mutex};

use thiserror::Error;

/// Errors emitted by `Transport` impls.
#[derive(Debug, Error)]
#[non_exhaustive]
pub enum TransportError {
    /// The underlying I/O failed (socket dead, network partition,
    /// etc.). Implementations should distinguish recoverable
    /// (reconnect) from permanent (auth rejected) failures via
    /// variant structure in their own error type, then map to this
    /// variant at the trait boundary if they don't want to expose
    /// implementation details.
    #[error("transport I/O error: {0}")]
    Io(String),

    /// The transport is closed and no further sends are accepted.
    /// Receivers should still drain any already-queued bytes via
    /// `try_recv` before considering the channel done.
    #[error("transport closed")]
    Closed,
}

/// Wire-byte channel for Phase 5 collaboration.
///
/// Implementations push and pull opaque byte blobs. The blobs ARE
/// Loro `LoroDoc::export(...)` snapshots / updates; the
/// transport doesn't interpret them. Framing (where one blob ends
/// and the next starts) is the implementation's responsibility.
pub trait Transport {
    /// Push `bytes` to the channel. Implementations may buffer
    /// internally; the call returns when the bytes are
    /// **queued for send**, not when the remote has received them.
    ///
    /// On error, the caller should treat the byte blob as unsent
    /// and re-queue (or surface the error to the user). The
    /// transport will not retry automatically — that policy lives
    /// at the layer above (Phase 5.5 design will pick reconnect
    /// semantics).
    fn send(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<(), TransportError>;

    /// Non-blocking receive. Returns `Some(bytes)` if a blob is
    /// queued, `None` otherwise. Implementations choose how often
    /// they poll the underlying channel; the contract is "if you
    /// have something, give it to me, else `None`."
    ///
    /// Returns `Err(TransportError::Closed)` only when the channel
    /// is permanently closed AND its internal queue is empty. A
    /// transient I/O failure during poll surfaces as
    /// `Err(TransportError::Io)`.
    fn try_recv(&mut self) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, TransportError>;
}

/// No-op `Transport` impl for tests + scaffolding.
///
/// Records every `send` call in an internal `Vec` (so tests can
/// assert what would have gone over the wire) and `try_recv`
/// returns `None` forever (no incoming bytes). Suitable for tests
/// that exercise `CollabSession::append_op` without needing a
/// real channel. For 2-peer round-trip tests, use
/// [`LoopbackTransport::pair`] instead — `NoopTransport` is for
/// "I just need a Transport-shaped object" cases.
#[derive(Debug, Default)]
pub struct NoopTransport {
    /// Bytes the consumer would have pushed to the wire. Tests
    /// inspect this to verify their `CollabSession` calls emit the
    /// expected number / shape of exports.
    pub sent: Vec<Vec<u8>>,
    closed: bool,
}

impl NoopTransport {
    /// Construct a fresh `NoopTransport` with an empty `sent` log
    /// and `closed = false`.
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self::default()
    }

    /// Mark the channel closed. Subsequent `send` calls return
    /// `TransportError::Closed`; `try_recv` returns `Closed` since
    /// nothing's queued.
    pub fn close(&mut self) {
        self.closed = true;
    }
}

impl Transport for NoopTransport {
    fn send(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<(), TransportError> {
        if self.closed {
            return Err(TransportError::Closed);
        }
        self.sent.push(bytes.to_vec());
        Ok(())
    }

    fn try_recv(&mut self) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, TransportError> {
        if self.closed {
            return Err(TransportError::Closed);
        }
        Ok(None)
    }
}

/// **Phase 5.5 V1 (2026-05-19):** in-process paired `Transport`
/// impl for 2-peer round-trip testing.
///
/// Constructed via [`LoopbackTransport::pair`]: returns two
/// endpoints `(a, b)` where `a.send(bytes)` lands in `b`'s recv
/// queue and vice versa. The two endpoints share two
/// `Arc<Mutex<VecDeque<Vec<u8>>>>` channels via interior
/// mutability — `Send + Sync` so they can also be used across
/// threads if a future test wants that.
///
/// ## Use case
///
/// Wire two `CollabSession`s together for tests like:
/// ```ignore
/// let (mut tx_a, mut tx_b) = LoopbackTransport::pair();
/// let session_a = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1))?;
/// let session_b = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(2))?;
/// // ... append ops on a, drain via tx_a.send / tx_b.try_recv ...
/// ```
///
/// CollabSession does NOT auto-flush to its attached Transport
/// in V1 (Phase 5.5 V2 will wire append → send). For now, tests
/// drive the flow manually: append, export_bytes, send via the
/// transport, recv on the other side, merge_bytes.
///
/// ## Close semantics
///
/// Each endpoint has its OWN `closed` flag (separate
/// `AtomicBool`). `a.close()` shuts down endpoint A — A's
/// `send` + `try_recv` both return `TransportError::Closed`. B
/// is unaffected: B can still `send` (bytes land in A's inbox
/// but A won't drain them) and `try_recv` (drains any bytes
/// already in B's inbox).
pub struct LoopbackTransport {
    /// Channel WE drain via `try_recv`. The peer's `send` writes
    /// here.
    inbox: Arc<Mutex<VecDeque<Vec<u8>>>>,
    /// Channel WE write to via `send`. The peer's `try_recv`
    /// drains here.
    outbox: Arc<Mutex<VecDeque<Vec<u8>>>>,
    /// Per-endpoint close flag. Atomic so `close(&self)` doesn't
    /// need `&mut`.
    closed: AtomicBool,
}

impl std::fmt::Debug for LoopbackTransport {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        f.debug_struct("LoopbackTransport")
            .field(
                "inbox_len",
                &self.inbox.lock().map(|q| q.len()).unwrap_or(0),
            )
            .field(
                "outbox_len",
                &self.outbox.lock().map(|q| q.len()).unwrap_or(0),
            )
            .field("closed", &self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed))
            .finish()
    }
}

impl LoopbackTransport {
    /// Construct a pair of `LoopbackTransport` endpoints. Returns
    /// `(a, b)` where `a.send(...)` enqueues to `b.try_recv()` and
    /// `b.send(...)` enqueues to `a.try_recv()`.
    pub fn pair() -> (Self, Self) {
        let a_to_b = Arc::new(Mutex::new(VecDeque::new()));
        let b_to_a = Arc::new(Mutex::new(VecDeque::new()));
        let a = Self {
            inbox: b_to_a.clone(),
            outbox: a_to_b.clone(),
            closed: AtomicBool::new(false),
        };
        let b = Self {
            inbox: a_to_b,
            outbox: b_to_a,
            closed: AtomicBool::new(false),
        };
        (a, b)
    }

    /// Mark this endpoint closed. Subsequent `send` / `try_recv`
    /// calls return `TransportError::Closed`. The peer endpoint
    /// is NOT affected by this call (each side has its own close
    /// flag).
    pub fn close(&self) {
        self.closed.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed);
    }

    /// True if this endpoint has been closed.
    pub fn is_closed(&self) -> bool {
        self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed)
    }

    /// Number of bytes blobs waiting in this endpoint's inbox
    /// (i.e. sent by the peer, not yet drained via `try_recv`).
    /// Useful for tests that want to assert "the peer sent N
    /// blobs to me" without consuming them.
    pub fn pending_recv(&self) -> usize {
        self.inbox.lock().map(|q| q.len()).unwrap_or(0)
    }
}

impl Transport for LoopbackTransport {
    fn send(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<(), TransportError> {
        if self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
            return Err(TransportError::Closed);
        }
        self.outbox
            .lock()
            .map_err(|e| TransportError::Io(format!("loopback outbox lock poisoned: {e}")))?
            .push_back(bytes.to_vec());
        Ok(())
    }

    fn try_recv(&mut self) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, TransportError> {
        if self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
            return Err(TransportError::Closed);
        }
        Ok(self
            .inbox
            .lock()
            .map_err(|e| TransportError::Io(format!("loopback inbox lock poisoned: {e}")))?
            .pop_front())
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::{LoopbackTransport, NoopTransport, Transport, TransportError};

    #[test]
    fn noop_send_records_bytes() {
        let mut t = NoopTransport::new();
        t.send(b"hello").unwrap();
        t.send(b"world").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(t.sent, vec![b"hello".to_vec(), b"world".to_vec()]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn noop_try_recv_returns_none_when_open() {
        let mut t = NoopTransport::new();
        assert!(matches!(t.try_recv(), Ok(None)));
    }

    #[test]
    fn noop_send_after_close_errors() {
        let mut t = NoopTransport::new();
        t.send(b"first").unwrap();
        t.close();
        assert!(matches!(t.send(b"second"), Err(TransportError::Closed)));
        // The first send still recorded.
        assert_eq!(t.sent, vec![b"first".to_vec()]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn noop_try_recv_after_close_errors() {
        let mut t = NoopTransport::new();
        t.close();
        assert!(matches!(t.try_recv(), Err(TransportError::Closed)));
    }

    // -- LoopbackTransport tests (Phase 5.5 V1) --

    #[test]
    fn loopback_pair_a_to_b_round_trip() {
        let (mut a, mut b) = LoopbackTransport::pair();
        a.send(b"hello").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(b.try_recv().unwrap(), Some(b"hello".to_vec()));
        assert_eq!(b.try_recv().unwrap(), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn loopback_pair_b_to_a_round_trip() {
        let (mut a, mut b) = LoopbackTransport::pair();
        b.send(b"world").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(a.try_recv().unwrap(), Some(b"world".to_vec()));
        assert_eq!(a.try_recv().unwrap(), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn loopback_pair_bidirectional() {
        let (mut a, mut b) = LoopbackTransport::pair();
        a.send(b"a1").unwrap();
        b.send(b"b1").unwrap();
        a.send(b"a2").unwrap();

        assert_eq!(b.try_recv().unwrap(), Some(b"a1".to_vec()));
        assert_eq!(a.try_recv().unwrap(), Some(b"b1".to_vec()));
        assert_eq!(b.try_recv().unwrap(), Some(b"a2".to_vec()));
        assert_eq!(a.try_recv().unwrap(), None);
        assert_eq!(b.try_recv().unwrap(), None);
    }

//! `CollabSession` — per-peer collaboration state holder.
//!
//! **Phase 5.2.a (2026-05-19, scaffold ship):** wraps `ql_oplog::OpLog`
//! with peer-id-aware operations + transport plumbing. Subsequent
//! phases populate richer behavior:
//!
//! - ~~**Phase 5.4**~~ ✅ V1 shipped at `89c02b9d83e` — per-peer
//!   Loro `UndoManager` tracks local appends. Undo APPENDS
//!   inverse ops + retracts originals from the visible `"ops"`
//!   list (NOT physical removal). Presence-origin commits
//!   excluded. See [`session::CollabSession::undo`].
//! - **Phase 5.5** — automatic transport integration: append a
//!   local op → broadcast bytes via [`crate::Transport::send`];
//!   poll [`crate::Transport::try_recv`] for remote bytes →
//!   `merge_bytes`.
//! - ~~**Phase 5.6**~~ ✅ V1 shipped at `c677e244704` — `presence`
//!   module + 4 `CollabSession` methods (`update_presence` /
//!   `peer_presence` / `clear_presence` / `peers_with_presence`).
//! - **Phase 5.7** — IDE binding: `CollabSession` becomes the engine
//!   handle that the IDE attaches to each open workbook.
//!
//! What ships in 5.2.a (5.2.b update 2026-05-19):
//!
//! - `CollabSession::new(peer_id)` — fresh session with an empty
//!   `OpLog` and the peer-id wired through to
//!   `LoroDoc::set_peer_id` (Phase 5.2.b — was a label-only stamp
//!   in 5.2.a). Returns `Result` because Loro's `set_peer_id` is
//!   fallible.
//! - `CollabSession::from_snapshot(peer_id, bytes)` — fork from a
//!   shared base snapshot (used by both peers in the D-4 probe
//!   pattern). The reborn session's peer id is `peer_id`; existing
//!   ops in the imported snapshot retain their original
//!   attribution.
//! - `append_op(&mut self, Op)` — local append; delegates to
//!   `OpLog::append`.
//! - `merge_bytes(&mut self, &[u8])` — pull a remote peer's
//!   snapshot via Loro's CRDT merge; delegates to
//!   `OpLog::merge_bytes`.
//! - `export_bytes(&self)` — produce a snapshot for transport.
//! - `op_log(&self)` — read-only view of the underlying log
//!   (for replay against a workbook).
//! - `peer_id(&self)` — the session's stable peer id.

use thiserror::Error;

use ql_oplog::{Op, OpLog, OpLogError, PRESENCE_COMMIT_ORIGIN};

use crate::peer::PeerId;
use crate::presence::{self, PresenceError, PresenceState};

/// Errors emitted by `CollabSession` operations.
#[derive(Debug, Error)]
#[non_exhaustive]
pub enum CollabSessionError {
    /// The underlying `OpLog` failed (Loro encode/decode, serde,
    /// etc.). Surfaces the wrapped `OpLogError` for diagnosis.
    #[error("op log error: {0}")]
    OpLog(#[from] OpLogError),

    /// A presence-layer call failed (serde, peer-key parse,
    /// underlying OpLog).
    #[error("presence error: {0}")]
    Presence(#[from] PresenceError),

    /// **Phase 5.4 V1:** a Loro `UndoManager` call (`undo` /
    /// `redo`) returned an error. Reachable when the underlying
    /// document is in an invalid state for the requested
    /// operation (e.g. mid-transaction).
    #[error("undo/redo error: {0}")]
    Undo(#[from] loro::LoroError),
}

/// Per-peer collaboration state holder.
///
/// Owns an `OpLog` + a stable `PeerId`. Tested with `NoopTransport`
/// at scaffold time; Phase 5.5 will add the live transport
/// integration via `Transport::send` / `try_recv`.
///
/// The session is the engine-side handle for one peer's view of a
/// shared workbook. Multiple peers each hold their own
/// `CollabSession`, exchange byte blobs via [`crate::Transport`],
/// and call `merge_bytes` to incorporate remote ops. All sessions
/// converge to the same `OpLog` content after exchange (per Loro's
/// CRDT determinism — Phase 5.1 audit Codex V1).
pub struct CollabSession {
    peer_id: PeerId,
    log: OpLog,
    /// Phase 5.4 V1 — peer-local undo/redo.
    ///
    /// Drop-order note (Codex 5.4 V1 audit LOW): Rust drops fields
    /// in DECLARATION order, so this drops LAST (after `log`).
    /// That is safe here because `loro::UndoManager` owns its own
    /// `LoroDoc` clone (`loro-internal::undo:166,670`) — it doesn't
    /// borrow from `log`'s doc and won't UAF when `log` drops first.
    undo: loro::UndoManager,
}

impl CollabSession {
    /// Construct a fresh session with an empty op log.
    ///
    /// Use this when no shared base snapshot exists yet (the first
    /// peer to open a brand-new workbook). The `peer_id` is wired
    /// through to the underlying `LoroDoc::set_peer_id` so Loro's
    /// CRDT merge metadata attributes this session's appends to
    /// `peer_id` (not Loro's default random per-doc id).
    ///
    /// **Caller pitfalls** (from `OpLog::set_peer_id`):
    /// - Two concurrent sessions MUST use distinct peer ids.
    ///   Duplicate ids corrupt the document via conflicting OpIDs.
    /// - Prefer per-process-random peer ids over user-/device-pinned
    ///   ids unless your transport layer enforces single-ownership.
    /// - `PeerId(u64::MAX)` is a Loro-reserved sentinel and returns
    ///   `CollabSessionError::OpLog`.
    pub fn new(peer_id: PeerId) -> Result<Self, CollabSessionError> {
        let mut log = OpLog::new();
        log.set_peer_id(peer_id.as_u64())?;
        let undo = make_undo_manager(&log);
        Ok(Self { peer_id, log, undo })
    }

    /// Construct a session by importing a shared snapshot. Use this
    /// when joining a session that already has history (the second+
    /// peer to open a collaborative workbook).
    ///
    /// `bytes` is a `LoroDoc::export(ExportMode::Snapshot)` produced
    /// by an earlier `CollabSession::export_bytes` call (typically
    /// from the workbook owner / first peer). After import, this
    /// session's peer id is set to `peer_id` — distinct from the
    /// peer ids carried by the already-imported ops. Imported ops
    /// retain their original peer-id attribution (Loro semantics);
    /// only this session's future appends carry `peer_id`.
    ///
    /// **Caller pitfalls** (same as `CollabSession::new`):
    /// - The new `peer_id` MUST differ from every peer already
    ///   represented in the imported snapshot AND from every other
    ///   concurrent session — see `OpLog::set_peer_id` for details.
    /// - `PeerId(u64::MAX)` is reserved.
    pub fn from_snapshot(peer_id: PeerId, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Self, CollabSessionError> {
        let mut log = OpLog::import_bytes(bytes)?;
        log.set_peer_id(peer_id.as_u64())?;
        let undo = make_undo_manager(&log);
        Ok(Self { peer_id, log, undo })
    }

    /// Stable peer id assigned at session creation.
    pub fn peer_id(&self) -> PeerId {
        self.peer_id
    }

    /// Append a local op. Delegates to `OpLog::append`. Phase 5.5
    /// will additionally push the appended bytes through the
    /// attached `Transport`; 5.2.a leaves transport integration to
    /// the caller.
    pub fn append_op(&mut self, op: Op) -> Result<(), CollabSessionError> {
        self.log.append(op)?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Merge a remote peer's snapshot into this session's log. Loro's
    /// CRDT preserves both peers' concurrent appends in deterministic
    /// causal order (Fugue/origin-based with peer-id tiebreaker per
    /// Phase 5.1 audit Codex V1). Returns the new `len()` after merge.
    pub fn merge_bytes(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<usize, CollabSessionError> {
        Ok(self.log.merge_bytes(bytes)?)
    }

    /// Produce a Loro snapshot blob suitable for transport to other
    /// peers OR for `.qbook/oplog.bin` persistence.
    pub fn export_bytes(&self) -> Result<Vec<u8>, CollabSessionError> {
        Ok(self.log.export_bytes()?)
    }

    /// Borrow the underlying `OpLog` for replay against a `Workbook`.
    ///
    /// Typical usage:
    /// ```ignore
    /// let session = CollabSession::from_snapshot(peer_id, &bytes)?;
    /// let mut wb = ql_storage::Workbook::new();
    /// let reg = ql_functions::default_registry();
    /// ql_oplog::replay_into(session.op_log(), &mut wb, &reg)?;
    /// ```
    pub fn op_log(&self) -> &OpLog {
        &self.log
    }

    /// Current number of ops in the log. Cheap (cached).
    pub fn op_count(&self) -> usize {
        self.log.len()
    }

    /// True iff the log has no ops (session just opened, no edits yet).
    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.log.is_empty()
    }

    /// **Phase 5.6 V1 (2026-05-19):** write this session's own
    /// presence state into the shared `"presence"` LoroMap.
    ///
    /// Uses the session's `PeerId` as the map key (16-hex
    /// `Display` form). Subsequent calls overwrite the previous
    /// value (LWW per peer); merges with other peers'
    /// presence writes preserve all distinct peers.
    pub fn update_presence(&mut self, state: PresenceState) -> Result<(), CollabSessionError> {
        let key = presence::peer_key(self.peer_id);
        let json = serde_json::to_string(&state).map_err(PresenceError::Serialize)?;
        self.log.presence_set(&key, &json)?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// **Phase 5.6 V1 (2026-05-19):** read a peer's most recent
    /// presence state. Returns `Ok(None)` if the peer has never
    /// updated its presence in this session (or has been removed
    /// via `clear_presence`).
    pub fn peer_presence(&self, peer: PeerId) -> Result<Option<PresenceState>, CollabSessionError> {
        let key = presence::peer_key(peer);
        let Some(json) = self.log.presence_get(&key)? else {
            return Ok(None);
        };
        let state: PresenceState =
            serde_json::from_str(&json).map_err(|source| PresenceError::Deserialize {
                peer_key: key,
                source,
            })?;
        Ok(Some(state))
    }

    /// **Phase 5.6 V1 (2026-05-19):** remove this session's own
    /// presence entry from the shared map. Use this when the
    /// peer leaves the session (window close, disconnect). After
    /// removal, other peers' `peer_presence(self_id)` returns
    /// `Ok(None)`.
    pub fn clear_presence(&mut self) -> Result<(), CollabSessionError> {
        let key = presence::peer_key(self.peer_id);
        self.log.presence_remove(&key)?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** undo this session's last
    /// local op. Loro's `UndoManager` semantically inverts it by
    /// appending an inverse op (NOT by physical removal). Returns
    /// `Ok(true)` if an undo stack item was consumed,
    /// `Ok(false)` if the stack was empty.
    ///
    /// Local-only per Loro's `UndoManager` contract — remote ops
    /// from other peers (merged via `merge_bytes`) are NOT
    /// affected.
    ///
    /// Presence updates are excluded from the undo stack by
    /// construction (see `CollabSession::new`), so cursor movements
    /// don't consume undo slots.
    pub fn undo(&mut self) -> Result<bool, CollabSessionError> {
        Ok(self.undo.undo()?)
    }

    /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** redo the last undone op.
    /// Returns `Ok(true)` if a redo stack item was consumed,
    /// `Ok(false)` if the stack was empty.
    pub fn redo(&mut self) -> Result<bool, CollabSessionError> {
        Ok(self.undo.redo()?)
    }

    /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** true iff the undo stack has
    /// at least one item.
    pub fn can_undo(&self) -> bool {
        self.undo.can_undo()
    }

    /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** true iff the redo stack has
    /// at least one item.
    pub fn can_redo(&self) -> bool {
        self.undo.can_redo()
    }

    /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** number of items currently
    /// on the undo stack.
    pub fn undo_count(&self) -> usize {
        self.undo.undo_count()
    }

    /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** number of items currently
    /// on the redo stack.
    pub fn redo_count(&self) -> usize {
        self.undo.redo_count()
    }

    /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** clear both undo and redo
    /// stacks. Use when starting a fresh logical session (e.g.
    /// opening a new workbook tab while reusing the
    /// `CollabSession` shell).
    pub fn clear_undo_stack(&self) {
        self.undo.clear();
    }

    /// **Phase 5.6 V1 (2026-05-19):** list every peer with a
    /// presence entry in the shared map.
    ///
    /// Order is Loro's iteration order. Sort the result if you
    /// need determinism.
    ///
    /// Returns `Err(CollabSessionError::Presence(PresenceError::PeerKeyParse))`
    /// if a key in the map can't be parsed as a `PeerId` (only
    /// reachable if a future writer uses an incompatible
    /// encoding).
    pub fn peers_with_presence(&self) -> Result<Vec<PeerId>, CollabSessionError> {
        let keys = self.log.presence_peers();
        let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(keys.len());
        for k in keys {
            out.push(presence::parse_peer_key(&k)?);
        }
        Ok(out)
    }
}

impl std::fmt::Debug for CollabSession {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        f.debug_struct("CollabSession")
            .field("peer_id", &self.peer_id)
            .field("op_count", &self.log.len())
            .field("undo_count", &self.undo.undo_count())
            .field("redo_count", &self.undo.redo_count())
            .finish()
    }
}

/// Construct + configure a Loro `UndoManager` bound to `log`'s
/// underlying doc.
///
/// Phase 5.4 V1 setup: register `PRESENCE_COMMIT_ORIGIN` as an
/// exclude prefix so cursor-movement commits don't fill the
/// undo stack. (Phase 5.6 V1 tags presence writes with that
/// origin via `OpLog::presence_set` / `presence_remove`.)
fn make_undo_manager(log: &OpLog) -> loro::UndoManager {
    let mut undo = log.new_undo_manager();
    undo.add_exclude_origin_prefix(PRESENCE_COMMIT_ORIGIN);
    undo
}

mod tests {

            sheet,
            row,
            col,
            value: CellWireValue::Number(n),
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn new_session_is_empty() {
        let s = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(s.peer_id(), PeerId::new(1));
        assert_eq!(s.op_count(), 0);
        assert!(s.is_empty());
    }

     1	//! `Transport` trait — wire-byte channel for Phase 5 collaboration.
     2	//!
     3	//! **Phase 5.2.a (2026-05-19, scaffold ship):** defines the trait
     4	//! shape that Phase 5.5 will implement (WebSocket or
     5	//! Server-Sent-Events or custom protocol — the choice is 5.5's
     6	//! call). Tests in the meantime use [`NoopTransport`] which
     7	//! discards all traffic.
     8	//!
     9	//! The contract is intentionally minimal:
    10	//!
    11	//! - `send(&mut self, bytes: &[u8])` — push bytes to the channel.
    12	//!   Bytes are an opaque blob (a Loro export). The transport
    13	//!   doesn't interpret them.
    14	//! - `try_recv(&mut self) -> Option<Vec<u8>>` — non-blocking
    15	//!   receive. Returns `None` if no bytes are queued.
    16	//!
    17	//! That's enough to plumb a `CollabSession::export_bytes` →
    18	//! transport → remote `CollabSession::merge_bytes` flow. The
    19	//! transport handles framing / reliability / reconnect / auth as
    20	//! its implementation details.

    22	use std::collections::VecDeque;

    28	/// Errors emitted by `Transport` impls.
    29	#[derive(Debug, Error)]
    30	#[non_exhaustive]
    31	pub enum TransportError {
    32	    /// The underlying I/O failed (socket dead, network partition,
    33	    /// etc.). Implementations should distinguish recoverable
    34	    /// (reconnect) from permanent (auth rejected) failures via
    35	    /// variant structure in their own error type, then map to this
    36	    /// variant at the trait boundary if they don't want to expose
    37	    /// implementation details.
    38	    #[error("transport I/O error: {0}")]
    39	    Io(String),
    40	
    41	    /// The transport is closed and no further sends are accepted.
    42	    /// Receivers should still drain any already-queued bytes via
    43	    /// `try_recv` before considering the channel done.
    44	    #[error("transport closed")]
    45	    Closed,
    46	}
    47	
    48	/// Wire-byte channel for Phase 5 collaboration.
    49	///
    50	/// Implementations push and pull opaque byte blobs. The blobs ARE
    51	/// Loro `LoroDoc::export(...)` snapshots / updates; the
    52	/// transport doesn't interpret them. Framing (where one blob ends
    53	/// and the next starts) is the implementation's responsibility.
    54	pub trait Transport {
    55	    /// Push `bytes` to the channel. Implementations may buffer
    56	    /// internally; the call returns when the bytes are
    57	    /// **queued for send**, not when the remote has received them.
    58	    ///
    59	    /// On error, the caller should treat the byte blob as unsent
    60	    /// and re-queue (or surface the error to the user). The
    61	    /// transport will not retry automatically — that policy lives
    62	    /// at the layer above (Phase 5.5 design will pick reconnect
    63	    /// semantics).
    64	    fn send(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<(), TransportError>;
    65	
    66	    /// Non-blocking receive. Returns `Some(bytes)` if a blob is
    67	    /// queued, `None` otherwise. Implementations choose how often
    68	    /// they poll the underlying channel; the contract is "if you
    69	    /// have something, give it to me, else `None`."
    70	    ///
    71	    /// Returns `Err(TransportError::Closed)` only when the channel
    72	    /// is permanently closed AND its internal queue is empty. A
    73	    /// transient I/O failure during poll surfaces as
    74	    /// `Err(TransportError::Io)`.
    75	    fn try_recv(&mut self) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, TransportError>;
    76	}
    77

   137	///
   138	/// ## Use case

   161	pub struct LoopbackTransport {

   165	    /// Channel WE write to via `send`. The peer's `try_recv`
   166	    /// drains here.
   167	    outbox: Arc<Mutex<VecDeque<Vec<u8>>>>,
   168	    /// Per-endpoint close flag. Atomic so `close(&self)` doesn't
   169	    /// need `&mut`.
   170	    closed: AtomicBool,
   171	}

   192	    /// `b.send(...)` enqueues to `a.try_recv()`.
   193	    pub fn pair() -> (Self, Self) {

     1	//! `CollabSession` — per-peer collaboration state holder.
     2	//!
     3	//! **Phase 5.2.a (2026-05-19, scaffold ship):** wraps `ql_oplog::OpLog`
     4	//! with peer-id-aware operations + transport plumbing. Subsequent
     5	//! phases populate richer behavior:
     6	//!
     7	//! - ~~**Phase 5.4**~~ ✅ V1 shipped at `89c02b9d83e` — per-peer
     8	//!   Loro `UndoManager` tracks local appends. Undo APPENDS
     9	//!   inverse ops + retracts originals from the visible `"ops"`
    10	//!   list (NOT physical removal). Presence-origin commits
    11	//!   excluded. See [`session::CollabSession::undo`].
    12	//! - **Phase 5.5** — automatic transport integration: append a
    13	//!   local op → broadcast bytes via [`crate::Transport::send`];
    14	//!   poll [`crate::Transport::try_recv`] for remote bytes →
    15	//!   `merge_bytes`.
    16	//! - ~~**Phase 5.6**~~ ✅ V1 shipped at `c677e244704` — `presence`
    17	//!   module + 4 `CollabSession` methods (`update_presence` /
    18	//!   `peer_presence` / `clear_presence` / `peers_with_presence`).
    19	//! - **Phase 5.7** — IDE binding: `CollabSession` becomes the engine
    20	//!   handle that the IDE attaches to each open workbook.
    21	//!
    22	//! What ships in 5.2.a (5.2.b update 2026-05-19):
    23	//!
    24	//! - `CollabSession::new(peer_id)` — fresh session with an empty
    25	//!   `OpLog` and the peer-id wired through to
    26	//!   `LoroDoc::set_peer_id` (Phase 5.2.b — was a label-only stamp
    27	//!   in 5.2.a). Returns `Result` because Loro's `set_peer_id` is
    28	//!   fallible.
    29	//! - `CollabSession::from_snapshot(peer_id, bytes)` — fork from a
    30	//!   shared base snapshot (used by both peers in the D-4 probe
    31	//!   pattern). The reborn session's peer id is `peer_id`; existing
    32	//!   ops in the imported snapshot retain their original
    33	//!   attribution.
    34	//! - `append_op(&mut self, Op)` — local append; delegates to
    35	//!   `OpLog::append`.
    36	//! - `merge_bytes(&mut self, &[u8])` — pull a remote peer's
    37	//!   snapshot via Loro's CRDT merge; delegates to
    38	//!   `OpLog::merge_bytes`.
    39	//! - `export_bytes(&self)` — produce a snapshot for transport.
    40	//! - `op_log(&self)` — read-only view of the underlying log
    41	//!   (for replay against a workbook).
    42	//! - `peer_id(&self)` — the session's stable peer id.
    43	
    44	use thiserror::Error;
    45	
    46	use ql_oplog::{Op, OpLog, OpLogError, PRESENCE_COMMIT_ORIGIN};
    47	
    48	use crate::peer::PeerId;
    49	use crate::presence::{self, PresenceError, PresenceState};
    50	
    51	/// Errors emitted by `CollabSession` operations.
    52	#[derive(Debug, Error)]
    53	#[non_exhaustive]
    54	pub enum CollabSessionError {
    55	    /// The underlying `OpLog` failed (Loro encode/decode, serde,
    56	    /// etc.). Surfaces the wrapped `OpLogError` for diagnosis.
    57	    #[error("op log error: {0}")]
    58	    OpLog(#[from] OpLogError),
    59	
    60	    /// A presence-layer call failed (serde, peer-key parse,
    61	    /// underlying OpLog).
    62	    #[error("presence error: {0}")]
    63	    Presence(#[from] PresenceError),
    64	
    65	    /// **Phase 5.4 V1:** a Loro `UndoManager` call (`undo` /
    66	    /// `redo`) returned an error. Reachable when the underlying
    67	    /// document is in an invalid state for the requested
    68	    /// operation (e.g. mid-transaction).
    69	    #[error("undo/redo error: {0}")]
    70	    Undo(#[from] loro::LoroError),
    71	}
    72	
    73	/// Per-peer collaboration state holder.
    74	///
    75	/// Owns an `OpLog` + a stable `PeerId`. Tested with `NoopTransport`
    76	/// at scaffold time; Phase 5.5 will add the live transport
    77	/// integration via `Transport::send` / `try_recv`.
    78	///
    79	/// The session is the engine-side handle for one peer's view of a
    80	/// shared workbook. Multiple peers each hold their own
    81	/// `CollabSession`, exchange byte blobs via [`crate::Transport`],
    82	/// and call `merge_bytes` to incorporate remote ops. All sessions
    83	/// converge to the same `OpLog` content after exchange (per Loro's
    84	/// CRDT determinism — Phase 5.1 audit Codex V1).
    85	pub struct CollabSession {
    86	    peer_id: PeerId,
    87	    log: OpLog,
    88	    /// Phase 5.4 V1 — peer-local undo/redo.
    89	    ///
    90	    /// Drop-order note (Codex 5.4 V1 audit LOW): Rust drops fields
    91	    /// in DECLARATION order, so this drops LAST (after `log`).
    92	    /// That is safe here because `loro::UndoManager` owns its own
    93	    /// `LoroDoc` clone (`loro-internal::undo:166,670`) — it doesn't
    94	    /// borrow from `log`'s doc and won't UAF when `log` drops first.
    95	    undo: loro::UndoManager,
    96	}
    97	
    98	impl CollabSession {
    99	    /// Construct a fresh session with an empty op log.
   100	    ///
   101	    /// Use this when no shared base snapshot exists yet (the first
   102	    /// peer to open a brand-new workbook). The `peer_id` is wired
   103	    /// through to the underlying `LoroDoc::set_peer_id` so Loro's
   104	    /// CRDT merge metadata attributes this session's appends to
   105	    /// `peer_id` (not Loro's default random per-doc id).
   106	    ///
   107	    /// **Caller pitfalls** (from `OpLog::set_peer_id`):
   108	    /// - Two concurrent sessions MUST use distinct peer ids.
   109	    ///   Duplicate ids corrupt the document via conflicting OpIDs.
   110	    /// - Prefer per-process-random peer ids over user-/device-pinned
   111	    ///   ids unless your transport layer enforces single-ownership.
   112	    /// - `PeerId(u64::MAX)` is a Loro-reserved sentinel and returns
   113	    ///   `CollabSessionError::OpLog`.
   114	    pub fn new(peer_id: PeerId) -> Result<Self, CollabSessionError> {
   115	        let mut log = OpLog::new();
   116	        log.set_peer_id(peer_id.as_u64())?;
   117	        let undo = make_undo_manager(&log);
   118	        Ok(Self { peer_id, log, undo })
   119	    }
   120	
   121	    /// Construct a session by importing a shared snapshot. Use this
   122	    /// when joining a session that already has history (the second+
   123	    /// peer to open a collaborative workbook).
   124	    ///
   125	    /// `bytes` is a `LoroDoc::export(ExportMode::Snapshot)` produced
   126	    /// by an earlier `CollabSession::export_bytes` call (typically
   127	    /// from the workbook owner / first peer). After import, this
   128	    /// session's peer id is set to `peer_id` — distinct from the
   129	    /// peer ids carried by the already-imported ops. Imported ops
   130	    /// retain their original peer-id attribution (Loro semantics);
   131	    /// only this session's future appends carry `peer_id`.
   132	    ///
   133	    /// **Caller pitfalls** (same as `CollabSession::new`):
   134	    /// - The new `peer_id` MUST differ from every peer already
   135	    ///   represented in the imported snapshot AND from every other
   136	    ///   concurrent session — see `OpLog::set_peer_id` for details.
   137	    /// - `PeerId(u64::MAX)` is reserved.
   138	    pub fn from_snapshot(peer_id: PeerId, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Self, CollabSessionError> {
   139	        let mut log = OpLog::import_bytes(bytes)?;
   140	        log.set_peer_id(peer_id.as_u64())?;
   141	        let undo = make_undo_manager(&log);
   142	        Ok(Self { peer_id, log, undo })
   143	    }
   144	
   145	    /// Stable peer id assigned at session creation.
   146	    pub fn peer_id(&self) -> PeerId {
   147	        self.peer_id
   148	    }
   149	
   150	    /// Append a local op. Delegates to `OpLog::append`. Phase 5.5
   151	    /// will additionally push the appended bytes through the
   152	    /// attached `Transport`; 5.2.a leaves transport integration to
   153	    /// the caller.
   154	    pub fn append_op(&mut self, op: Op) -> Result<(), CollabSessionError> {
   155	        self.log.append(op)?;
   156	        Ok(())
   157	    }
   158	
   159	    /// Merge a remote peer's snapshot into this session's log. Loro's
   160	    /// CRDT preserves both peers' concurrent appends in deterministic
   161	    /// causal order (Fugue/origin-based with peer-id tiebreaker per
   162	    /// Phase 5.1 audit Codex V1). Returns the new `len()` after merge.
   163	    pub fn merge_bytes(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<usize, CollabSessionError> {
   164	        Ok(self.log.merge_bytes(bytes)?)
   165	    }
   166	
   167	    /// Produce a Loro snapshot blob suitable for transport to other
   168	    /// peers OR for `.qbook/oplog.bin` persistence.
   169	    pub fn export_bytes(&self) -> Result<Vec<u8>, CollabSessionError> {
   170	        Ok(self.log.export_bytes()?)
   171	    }
   172	
   173	    /// Borrow the underlying `OpLog` for replay against a `Workbook`.
   174	    ///
   175	    /// Typical usage:
   176	    /// ```ignore
   177	    /// let session = CollabSession::from_snapshot(peer_id, &bytes)?;
   178	    /// let mut wb = ql_storage::Workbook::new();
   179	    /// let reg = ql_functions::default_registry();
   180	    /// ql_oplog::replay_into(session.op_log(), &mut wb, &reg)?;
   181	    /// ```
   182	    pub fn op_log(&self) -> &OpLog {
   183	        &self.log
   184	    }
   185	
   186	    /// Current number of ops in the log. Cheap (cached).
   187	    pub fn op_count(&self) -> usize {
   188	        self.log.len()
   189	    }
   190	
   191	    /// True iff the log has no ops (session just opened, no edits yet).
   192	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
   193	        self.log.is_empty()
   194	    }
   195	
   196	    /// **Phase 5.6 V1 (2026-05-19):** write this session's own
   197	    /// presence state into the shared `"presence"` LoroMap.
   198	    ///
   199	    /// Uses the session's `PeerId` as the map key (16-hex
   200	    /// `Display` form). Subsequent calls overwrite the previous
   201	    /// value (LWW per peer); merges with other peers'
   202	    /// presence writes preserve all distinct peers.
   203	    pub fn update_presence(&mut self, state: PresenceState) -> Result<(), CollabSessionError> {
   204	        let key = presence::peer_key(self.peer_id);
   205	        let json = serde_json::to_string(&state).map_err(PresenceError::Serialize)?;
   206	        self.log.presence_set(&key, &json)?;
   207	        Ok(())
   208	    }
   209	
   210	    /// **Phase 5.6 V1 (2026-05-19):** read a peer's most recent
   211	    /// presence state. Returns `Ok(None)` if the peer has never
   212	    /// updated its presence in this session (or has been removed
   213	    /// via `clear_presence`).
   214	    pub fn peer_presence(&self, peer: PeerId) -> Result<Option<PresenceState>, CollabSessionError> {
   215	        let key = presence::peer_key(peer);
   216	        let Some(json) = self.log.presence_get(&key)? else {
   217	            return Ok(None);
   218	        };
   219	        let state: PresenceState =
   220	            serde_json::from_str(&json).map_err(|source| PresenceError::Deserialize {
   221	                peer_key: key,
   222	                source,
   223	            })?;
   224	        Ok(Some(state))
   225	    }
   226	
   227	    /// **Phase 5.6 V1 (2026-05-19):** remove this session's own
   228	    /// presence entry from the shared map. Use this when the
   229	    /// peer leaves the session (window close, disconnect). After
   230	    /// removal, other peers' `peer_presence(self_id)` returns
   231	    /// `Ok(None)`.
   232	    pub fn clear_presence(&mut self) -> Result<(), CollabSessionError> {
   233	        let key = presence::peer_key(self.peer_id);
   234	        self.log.presence_remove(&key)?;
   235	        Ok(())
   236	    }
   237	
   238	    /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** undo this session's last
   239	    /// local op. Loro's `UndoManager` semantically inverts it by
   240	    /// appending an inverse op (NOT by physical removal). Returns
   241	    /// `Ok(true)` if an undo stack item was consumed,
   242	    /// `Ok(false)` if the stack was empty.
   243	    ///
   244	    /// Local-only per Loro's `UndoManager` contract — remote ops
   245	    /// from other peers (merged via `merge_bytes`) are NOT
   246	    /// affected.
   247	    ///
   248	    /// Presence updates are excluded from the undo stack by
   249	    /// construction (see `CollabSession::new`), so cursor movements
   250	    /// don't consume undo slots.
   251	    pub fn undo(&mut self) -> Result<bool, CollabSessionError> {
   252	        Ok(self.undo.undo()?)
   253	    }
   254	
   255	    /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** redo the last undone op.
   256	    /// Returns `Ok(true)` if a redo stack item was consumed,
   257	    /// `Ok(false)` if the stack was empty.
   258	    pub fn redo(&mut self) -> Result<bool, CollabSessionError> {
   259	        Ok(self.undo.redo()?)
   260	    }
   261	
   262	    /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** true iff the undo stack has
   263	    /// at least one item.
   264	    pub fn can_undo(&self) -> bool {
   265	        self.undo.can_undo()
   266	    }
   267	
   268	    /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** true iff the redo stack has
   269	    /// at least one item.
   270	    pub fn can_redo(&self) -> bool {
   271	        self.undo.can_redo()
   272	    }
   273	
   274	    /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** number of items currently
   275	    /// on the undo stack.
   276	    pub fn undo_count(&self) -> usize {
   277	        self.undo.undo_count()
   278	    }
   279	
   280	    /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** number of items currently
   281	    /// on the redo stack.
   282	    pub fn redo_count(&self) -> usize {
   283	        self.undo.redo_count()
   284	    }
   285	
   286	    /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** clear both undo and redo
   287	    /// stacks. Use when starting a fresh logical session (e.g.
   288	    /// opening a new workbook tab while reusing the
   289	    /// `CollabSession` shell).
   290	    pub fn clear_undo_stack(&self) {
   291	        self.undo.clear();
   292	    }
   293	
   294	    /// **Phase 5.6 V1 (2026-05-19):** list every peer with a
   295	    /// presence entry in the shared map.
   296	    ///
   297	    /// Order is Loro's iteration order. Sort the result if you
   298	    /// need determinism.
   299	    ///
   300	    /// Returns `Err(CollabSessionError::Presence(PresenceError::PeerKeyParse))`
   301	    /// if a key in the map can't be parsed as a `PeerId` (only
   302	    /// reachable if a future writer uses an incompatible
   303	    /// encoding).
   304	    pub fn peers_with_presence(&self) -> Result<Vec<PeerId>, CollabSessionError> {
   305	        let keys = self.log.presence_peers();
   306	        let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(keys.len());
   307	        for k in keys {
   308	            out.push(presence::parse_peer_key(&k)?);
   309	        }
   310	        Ok(out)
   311	    }
   312	}
   313	
   314	impl std::fmt::Debug for CollabSession {
   315	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
   316	        f.debug_struct("CollabSession")
   317	            .field("peer_id", &self.peer_id)
   318	            .field("op_count", &self.log.len())
   319	            .field("undo_count", &self.undo.undo_count())
   320	            .field("redo_count", &self.undo.redo_count())
   321	            .finish()
   322	    }
   323	}
   324	
   325	/// Construct + configure a Loro `UndoManager` bound to `log`'s
   326	/// underlying doc.
   327	///
   328	/// Phase 5.4 V1 setup: register `PRESENCE_COMMIT_ORIGIN` as an
   329	/// exclude prefix so cursor-movement commits don't fill the
   330	/// undo stack. (Phase 5.6 V1 tags presence writes with that
   331	/// origin via `OpLog::presence_set` / `presence_remove`.)
   332	fn make_undo_manager(log: &OpLog) -> loro::UndoManager {
   333	    let mut undo = log.new_undo_manager();
   334	    undo.add_exclude_origin_prefix(PRESENCE_COMMIT_ORIGIN);
   335	    undo
   336	}
   337	
   338	#[cfg(test)]
   339	mod tests {
   340	    use super::*;
   341	    use ql_oplog::CellWireValue;
   342	
   343	    fn put_value(sheet: u16, row: u32, col: u32, n: f64) -> Op {
   344	        Op::PutValue {
   345	            sheet,
   346	            row,
   347	            col,
   348	            value: CellWireValue::Number(n),
   349	        }
   350	    }
   351	
   352	    #[test]
   353	    fn new_session_is_empty() {
   354	        let s = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
   355	        assert_eq!(s.peer_id(), PeerId::new(1));
   356	        assert_eq!(s.op_count(), 0);
   357	        assert!(s.is_empty());
   358	    }
   359	
   360	    #[test]
   361	    fn append_grows_log() {
   362	        let mut s = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
   363	        s.append_op(Op::AddSheet {
   364	            name: "S".to_owned(),
   365	            chunk_rows: 16384,
   366	        })
   367	        .unwrap();
   368	        s.append_op(put_value(0, 0, 0, 42.0)).unwrap();
   369	        assert_eq!(s.op_count(), 2);
   370	        assert!(!s.is_empty());
   371	    }
   372	
   373	    #[test]
   374	    fn from_snapshot_reconstructs_op_count() {
   375	        let mut origin = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
   376	        origin
   377	            .append_op(Op::AddSheet {
   378	                name: "S".to_owned(),
   379	                chunk_rows: 16384,
   380	            })
   381	            .unwrap();
   382	        origin.append_op(put_value(0, 0, 0, 1.0)).unwrap();
   383	
   384	        let bytes = origin.export_bytes().unwrap();
   385	        let reborn = CollabSession::from_snapshot(PeerId::new(2), &bytes).unwrap();
   386	        assert_eq!(reborn.peer_id(), PeerId::new(2));
   387	        assert_eq!(reborn.op_count(), 2);
   388	    }
   389	
   390	    #[test]
   391	    fn merge_bytes_grows_log_with_remote_ops() {
   392	        // Two peers, shared base, each appends; one merges the other.
   393	        let mut base = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(100)).unwrap();
   394	        base.append_op(Op::AddSheet {
   395	            name: "S".to_owned(),
   396	            chunk_rows: 16384,
   397	        })
   398	        .unwrap();
   399	        let base_bytes = base.export_bytes().unwrap();
   400	
   401	        let mut peer_a = CollabSession::from_snapshot(PeerId::new(1), &base_bytes).unwrap();
   402	        peer_a.append_op(put_value(0, 0, 0, 10.0)).unwrap();
   403	
   404	        let mut peer_b = CollabSession::from_snapshot(PeerId::new(2), &base_bytes).unwrap();
   405	        peer_b.append_op(put_value(0, 1, 0, 20.0)).unwrap();
   406	
   407	        // Peer A pulls peer B's bytes.
   408	        let b_bytes = peer_b.export_bytes().unwrap();
   409	        let merged_count = peer_a.merge_bytes(&b_bytes).unwrap();
   410	        assert!(
   411	            merged_count >= 3,
   412	            "merged log has at least AddSheet + 2× PutValue (got {merged_count})"
   413	        );
   414	    }
   415	
   416	    #[test]
   417	    fn debug_impl_includes_peer_id_and_count() {
   418	        let mut s = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(42)).unwrap();
   419	        s.append_op(put_value(0, 0, 0, 1.0)).ok();
   420	        let d = format!("{s:?}");
   421	        assert!(d.contains("peer_id"), "Debug must include peer_id: {d}");
   422	        assert!(d.contains("op_count"), "Debug must include op_count: {d}");
   423	    }
   424	
   425	    #[test]
   426	    fn peer_id_is_wired_to_underlying_loro_doc() {
   427	        // Regression: prior to Phase 5.2.b the PeerId was stored on
   428	        // CollabSession but never passed to LoroDoc::set_peer_id, so
   429	        // op attribution at the CRDT layer used Loro's random
   430	        // per-doc peer id. After 5.2.b the configured PeerId MUST
   431	        // appear as the underlying log's peer_id().
   432	        let s = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(0xdead_beef_cafe_babe)).unwrap();
   433	        assert_eq!(s.peer_id().as_u64(), 0xdead_beef_cafe_babe);
   434	        assert_eq!(s.op_log().peer_id(), 0xdead_beef_cafe_babe);
   435	    }
   436	
   437	    #[test]
   438	    fn from_snapshot_overrides_imported_peer_id() {
   439	        // The origin session writes ops under PeerId(11). The reborn
   440	        // session imports the snapshot but uses its own PeerId(22)
   441	        // for future appends — the imported ops keep their original
   442	        // attribution (Loro semantics), but log.peer_id() reflects
   443	        // the reborn's configured PeerId.
   444	        //
   445	        // Codex/Opus 5.2.b audit caught: the prior version exported
   446	        // an empty origin, so the "imported ops retain peer 11"
   447	        // promise was never exercised. Now we append before export
   448	        // and assert the imported op survives the peer-id change.
   449	        let mut origin = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(11)).unwrap();
   450	        origin
   451	            .append_op(Op::AddSheet {
   452	                name: "S".to_owned(),
   453	                chunk_rows: 16384,
   454	            })
   455	            .unwrap();
   456	        let bytes = origin.export_bytes().unwrap();
   457	
   458	        let mut reborn = CollabSession::from_snapshot(PeerId::new(22), &bytes).unwrap();
   459	        assert_eq!(reborn.op_log().peer_id(), 22);
   460	        assert_eq!(
   461	            reborn.op_count(),
   462	            1,
   463	            "imported op must survive peer-id swap"
   464	        );
   465	        // Reborn can append under its new peer id.
   466	        reborn.append_op(put_value(0, 0, 0, 99.0)).unwrap();
   467	        assert_eq!(reborn.op_count(), 2);
   468	        assert_eq!(reborn.op_log().peer_id(), 22);
   469	    }
   470	
   471	    #[test]
   472	    fn presence_round_trip_within_one_session() {
   473	        let mut s = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(7)).unwrap();
   474	        // No presence yet.
   475	        assert_eq!(s.peer_presence(PeerId::new(7)).unwrap(), None);
   476	        assert!(s.peers_with_presence().unwrap().is_empty());
   477	
   478	        // Set our own.
   479	        let state = PresenceState {
   480	            sheet: 1,
   481	            row: 10,
   482	            col: 5,
   483	            selection_end_row: 12,
   484	            selection_end_col: 7,
   485	            typing: true,
   486	        };
   487	        s.update_presence(state).unwrap();
   488	
   489	        let read = s.peer_presence(PeerId::new(7)).unwrap().unwrap();
   490	        assert_eq!(read, state);
   491	        let peers = s.peers_with_presence().unwrap();
   492	        assert_eq!(peers, vec![PeerId::new(7)]);
   493	
   494	        // Overwrite (LWW per key).
   495	        let state2 = PresenceState::at_cell(0, 0, 0);

   498	        assert_eq!(read2, state2);
   499	        assert_eq!(s.peers_with_presence().unwrap().len(), 1);
   500	
   501	        // Clear.
   502	        s.clear_presence().unwrap();
   503	        assert_eq!(s.peer_presence(PeerId::new(7)).unwrap(), None);
   504	        assert!(s.peers_with_presence().unwrap().is_empty());
   505	    }

   783	    #[test]
   784	    fn two_sessions_exchange_state_via_loopback_transport() {
   785	        // Phase 5.5 V1 integration: drive two CollabSessions
   786	        // through a LoopbackTransport pair. V1 doesn't auto-
   787	        // flush appends to the transport (5.5 V2 work), so the
   788	        // test manually exports/sends/recvs/merges to verify the
   789	        // wire actually carries the right bytes.
   790	        use crate::transport::{LoopbackTransport, Transport};
   791	        let (mut tx_a, mut tx_b) = LoopbackTransport::pair();
   792	
   793	        let base = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
   794	        let base_bytes = base.export_bytes().unwrap();
   795	
   796	        let mut peer_a = CollabSession::from_snapshot(PeerId::new(0xa), &base_bytes).unwrap();
   797	        let mut peer_b = CollabSession::from_snapshot(PeerId::new(0xb), &base_bytes).unwrap();
   798	
   799	        // Peer A appends an op, exports, sends over the wire.
   800	        peer_a.append_op(put_value(0, 0, 0, 42.0)).unwrap();
   801	        let a_bytes = peer_a.export_bytes().unwrap();
   802	        tx_a.send(&a_bytes).unwrap();
   803	
   804	        // Peer B drains the transport and merges.
   805	        let received = tx_b
   806	            .try_recv()
   807	            .unwrap()
   808	            .expect("tx_b must receive A's bytes");
   809	        assert_eq!(received, a_bytes);
   810	        peer_b.merge_bytes(&received).unwrap();
   811	        assert!(peer_b.op_count() >= 1, "B must see A's op after merge");
   812	
   813	        // Reverse direction: B appends, sends to A.
   814	        peer_b.append_op(put_value(0, 1, 0, 99.0)).unwrap();
   815	        tx_b.send(&peer_b.export_bytes().unwrap()).unwrap();
   816	        let b_received = tx_a
   817	            .try_recv()
   818	            .unwrap()
   819	            .expect("tx_a must receive B's bytes");
   820	        peer_a.merge_bytes(&b_received).unwrap();
   821	
   822	        // After the exchange, both sessions converge on the same op count.
   823	        assert_eq!(peer_a.op_count(), peer_b.op_count());
   824	    }

 succeeded in 266ms:
   360	cursor movement doesn't pollute the undo stack.
   361	
   362	### V1 limitations / V2 follow-ups
   363	
   364	- No grouping API (`group_start` / `group_end`) — defer to V2.
   365	- No merge-interval tuning (Loro's default 0 ms) — defer to V2.
   366	- No push/pop listeners — defer to V2.
   367	- `set_peer_id` after construction silently CLEARS the undo
   368	  stack per Loro's internal subscription. V1 made
   369	  `OpLog::set_peer_id` `&mut self` so the footgun isn't
   370	  reachable from a shared `&OpLog` (Codex+Opus 5.4 V1 audit
   371	  closure).
   372	
   373	## Transport (Phase 5.5 preview)
   374	
   375	Loro's wire format:
   376	- `LoroDoc::export(ExportMode::Updates(version_vector))` →
   377	  bytes carrying only ops new-to-the-peer-since-version-vector.
   378	- `LoroDoc::import(bytes)` → merge into local doc.
   379	
   380	Phase 5.5 wraps these with a WebSocket transport (or any
   381	bidirectional byte channel). Each peer pushes version-vector
   382	updates to the server; server fan-outs to other peers; each
   383	peer imports.
   384	
   385	5.1 does NOT pick a transport (WS vs Server-Sent Events vs
   386	custom protocol) — that's 5.5's call. 5.1 just confirms the
   387	substrate works regardless.
   388	
   389	## Backwards compatibility with `.qbook` envelope
   390	
   391	The current persistence layer (`crates/ql-io`) saves the
   392	workbook as a `.qbook/` directory with JSON manifests +
   393	parquet cell data. Op log persistence (`ql-oplog::persistence`)
   394	saves the LoroDoc snapshot as `oplog.bin`.
   395	
   396	Phase 5 preserves both. The CRDT additions are purely additive:
   397	- `.qbook/oplog.bin` remains the same Loro snapshot blob.
   398	- Loading a `.qbook` still produces a single-writer-equivalent
   399	  workbook (no peer IDs, no presence).
   400	- Joining a session = load `.qbook` locally + receive Loro
   401	  updates from teammates + merge into the local doc.
   402	
   403	The pre-existing replay path handles the single-writer case;
   404	the new transport path handles the multi-peer case. Both call
   405	the same `replay_into` internally.
   406	
   407	## `ql-collab` crate scope
   408	
   409	**Phase 5.2.a (2026-05-19, ✅ shipped at `66a571b30af`):** scaffold
   410	ships with the following module surface:
   411	
   412	```rust
   413	// crates/ql-collab/src/lib.rs (current state)
   414	
   415	pub mod peer;        // PeerId newtype (u64 wrapper)
   416	pub mod session;     // CollabSession: per-peer state holder
   417	pub mod transport;   // trait Transport + NoopTransport test impl
   418	
   419	pub use peer::PeerId;
   420	pub use session::{CollabSession, CollabSessionError};
   421	pub use transport::{NoopTransport, Transport, TransportError};
   422	```
   423	
   424	Reserved (not yet populated, named in `docs/MASTER-PLAN.md` §541):
   425	- `presence` — Phase 5.6 cursor + selection map.
   426	- `undo` — Phase 5.4 peer-local undo via Loro `UndoManager`.
   427	
   428	Phase 5.2.a totals ~11 unit tests + ~600 LOC of scaffold. Phase
   429	5.2.b onward populates richer behavior; full Phase 5.2 crate LOC
   430	estimate remains 1,500-2,500 LOC + tests.

For a workbook with N formulas each registering M range deps spanning K stripe keys per range, memory grows as `O(N · M · K)` — bounded but unbenchmarked. Codex W5-49 listed this as a stop-condition for Option A. Acceptance: bench fixture in `crates/ql-bench` measuring memory growth as a function of N for N ∈ {1k, 10k, 100k} formulas with realistic range-dep distributions. | `crates/ql-calcgraph/src/stripes.rs` `formula_to_stripe_keys: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<StripeKey>>` field has no bench coverage | Engine | Engine Phase 4.10 (function library wave 2) or earlier if scale workload surfaces it |

The main implementation is simple: `send` locks only `outbox`, `try_recv` locks only `inbox`, and `close` only touches the endpoint-local atomic. I’ve found doc drift in the phase docs and module/session comments; now I’m running the requested gates from `quantbook-engine/`.

codex
**Findings**

| Severity | Location | Finding | Concrete fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| HIGH | none | No ship-blocking correctness issue found. | none |
| MEDIUM | `crates/ql-collab/src/transport.rs:41`, `:71`, `:243` | Public `Transport` docs say closed receivers should drain queued bytes / `Closed` only after empty, but `LoopbackTransport::try_recv` returns `Closed` immediately after `close()`, even with queued bytes. | Pick one contract. Given V1 tests intentionally block own-side recv after close, update trait/error docs to permit immediate endpoint closure, or change Loopback to drain-before-Closed and adjust tests. |
| LOW | `docs/architecture/crdt-data-model.md:373`, `docs/phase5/entry-plan.md:110`, `docs/known-gaps.md:116`, `crates/ql-collab/src/session.rs:12`, `crates/ql-collab/src/transport.rs:3` | Docs still describe Phase 5.5 as future / Noop-only. | Mark LoopbackTransport V1 shipped at `924750819bc`; leave WebSocket/offline sync as Phase 5.5 V2/future. |
| LOW | `crates/ql-collab/src/transport.rs:128` | Code claims `Send + Sync` but has no compile-time assertion. Implementation is Send+Sync by fields. | Add `fn assert_send_sync<T: Send + Sync>() {}` test for `LoopbackTransport`; optional thread-spawn smoke. |

**A. Concurrency**

A1 PASS. `LoopbackTransport` is genuinely `Send + Sync`: `Arc<Mutex<VecDeque<Vec<u8>>>>` is Send+Sync because `VecDeque<Vec<u8>>: Send`; `AtomicBool` is Send+Sync.

A2 PASS for memory safety. `Relaxed` is fine because the atomic is only a local closed flag and does not publish queue data; queue data is protected by `Mutex`. Acquire/Release would not solve a logical concurrent close/send race. If strict serialized close semantics are required, close must share the same synchronization protocol as send.

A3 PASS. Mapping poisoned locks to `TransportError::Io` is defensible for a non-panicking transport trait. Tests that unwrap will still fail loudly.

A4 PASS. No method locks both queues. `send` locks only `outbox`; `try_recv` and `pending_recv` lock only `inbox`; `Debug` computes lengths sequentially without holding both guards.

**B. API**

B1 PASS. Keeping `&mut self` is defensible: it matches the trait, `NoopTransport`, and likely future socket-backed transports.

B2 PASS with caveat. Endpoint-local close is documented and tested: B can still send after A closes, creating orphaned bytes. That is acceptable for an in-process test transport, but not production close semantics.

B3 PASS. `close(&self)` / `is_closed(&self)` with trait `send/try_recv(&mut self)` is reasonable because close is just an atomic endpoint flag.

B4 PASS implementation, LOW test gap. Add static Send+Sync assertion.

**C. Coverage**

C1 Mostly PASS. Core FIFO, bidirectional, close, pending, debug covered.

C2 PASS. No 1MB blob test needed for V1; the path is just `bytes.to_vec()` into `VecDeque`.

C3 SHOULD-FIX. A thread-spawn test is useful but compile-time Send+Sync assertion is enough for the claim.

C4 SHOULD-FIX. Session integration covers byte delivery and op-count convergence; stronger content-level convergence would be better than `op_count` only.

C5 PASS. Already covered at `transport.rs:344-355`: B sends successfully after A closes.

**D. Docs**

D1 FAIL doc cleanup. `crdt-data-model.md` still says Phase 5.5 preview.

D2 FAIL doc cleanup. `entry-plan.md` row 5.5 still says only “WebSocket + reconnect merge.”

D3 FAIL doc cleanup. `known-gaps.md` GAP-C-05 still says only `NoopTransport`.

D4 FAIL doc cleanup. `session.rs` still describes Phase 5.5 as future automatic integration.

D5 FAIL doc cleanup. `transport.rs` module doc still says Phase 5.5 “will implement” transport and tests use Noop in the meantime.

**E. Gates**

E1 PASS. `cargo test --workspace` passed. Current counted workspace total is 4349 “running N tests” entries, not the prompt’s 4196, so the expected count is stale for this checkout.

E2 PASS. `cargo fmt --all -- --check` clean.

E3 PASS. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean.

Phase 5.5 V1 is ship-clean, 3 SHOULD-FIX for cleanup.
